Potential algorithms:

A) **Active constraint projection / Frank-Wolfe**:
We minimize ∇max t. We can approximate by maintaining weights λ_k ≥0 on t_k, ∑λ=1, λ supported on near-max t. Then minimize surrogate Q(a)=∑λ_k t_k subject a≥0, ∑a=1. Q is quadratic (possibly non-convex). But if λ are probabilities on sums, Q = a^*T? Actually Q(a) = ∑_{k} λ_k ∑_i a_i a_{k-i}. This is a quadratic form with symmetric matrix B(a? depending on λ). We can maybe optimize via conjugate? Not convex. But we can do coordinate descent? For each i, t contributions.

B) **Alternating f/g LP**:
Given f, LP for g: maximize ∑g subject f*g ≤ M, g≥0, normalize. If solved, update f=(1−t)f+t g. This is like finding g that spreads mass in directions not causing high f*g. Could solve LP using scipy or custom because matrix F is convolution. Since f≥0, constraints are (f*g)_k ≤ M. M current max f*f. We can use scipy.optimize.linprog if n moderate (e.g., 500-2000). But budget and 6121? 6121 variables, 12241 constraints; linprog probably too slow. But we can use dual or projection.

LP form: max 1^T g s.t. A g ≤ M 1, g≥0. A is convolution with f (size (2n-1)×n) nonnegative. Dual: min M 1^T u s.t. A^T u ≥ 1, u≥0. Here A^T u_i = ∑_{k} u_k f_{k-i} = (f * u^reverse)? Essentially convolution. We need solve nonnegative u minimizing M∑u subject to (convolve f, reversed u)≥1. Could be easier? n constraints (for g) vs 2n, variables 2n. Still LP. But we can use simple active set? Or linear programming not necessary: The dual has form find nonnegative u with ∑(f shifted by u) ≥1, minimizing sum u. This is a covering LP. Since f nonnegative, can solve greedily? At optimum, support of u maybe active constraints. But maybe use subgradient.

Actually if we set u_k = t_k / M? Then A^T? No. If f*g is capped M, the max scaling of g maybe related to inverse of convolution operator. Need turn to algorithmic solution.

We can leverage **scipy.optimize** for a moderate-dimensional problem. Let's consider optimizing continuous f with basis of piecewise linear or Fourier and using direct objective with FFT. We can then upsample to length n for final. If 2D optimization fails, we can do direct local search on n=200.

Potential optimization methods:
1. **Nelder-Mead / Covariance Matrix Adaptation** on parameters (e.g., coefficients of polynomial or spline), objective continuous approximate with n=200. CMA not in scipy? `scipy.optimize.differential_evolution`, `dual_annealing` allow black-box. 1 CPU, 2400s, can do thousands evals if n small (200 conv via FFT or direct). Could find low R. But objective nonconvex; global search maybe.

2. **Particle swarm / random hill climbing** on piecewise values. We can implement our own.

3. **Direct coordinate descent** on vector a length ~100-500. Because objective scale invariant, we can fix sum=1. Use randomized coordinate line search with active top bins. This may converge to local with R maybe <1.5.

Let's design a robust hybrid:
- Start from several seeds: uniform, geometric, random smooth, height_sequence_1 (clip to [0,1000], normalize), maybe best known returned.
- For each seed, perform local optimization using **top-L active set gradient/subgradient** and **coordinate swaps**.
- Evaluate exact R at checkpoints; keep best list.

Time budget 2400s. Need ensure return valid. Could use n maybe 4096 or 8192. FFT convolution O(n log n) ~ 8192*13=1e5 per eval; thousands evals fine. But local search needs many convolutions. We can use `np.convolve` direct for n<=2000? 2n central O(n^2); for n=2000 direct 4M maybe okay but many times not. Use FFT for speed. But objective evaluation in final can use direct exact?    v = a - η G

Let's design a function `multiplicative_iterations(a, n, iter, K, eta, tau)` returns best.
Pseudo:
```
def conv_fft(x): # nonnegative real length n, full conv approx
    N = 1 << (2*n-1).bit_length()
    fx = np.fft.rfft(x, N)
    y = np.fft.irFFT(fx*fx, N)[:2*n-1]
    return y (maybe small negative set 0)
```
Since a nonnegative, convolution nonnegative; FFT errors small. Use for search.
Objective `score = 2*n*max(y)` sum normalized to1. If sum not1, normalize final.
But during multiplicative, sum1, y sum ~1.

Update:
```
g_scale = H.max() or mean

# if too many zeros? add eps? For search maybe floor to prevent all mass edges. We can rescale back: a += eps; normalize.

```
But H may be zero for some positions never in top bins; they won't decrease. To avoid mass accumulating at endpoints causing peaks, include a small uniform background λ: add `alpha * ones/(2n-1)` or small uniform to top weights so all positions connected. Use λ = (1-β)*top_weights + β*uniform over all bins. Then H positive everywhere. Choose β=0.05. Important.

Also active top selection should include weighted chance maybe random to escape.

Step size: Since F homogeneous, if we decrease some a, max may drop; too large may increase others. We can use backtracking: after update, compute new score; if worse, reduce η or mix with parent. But computing score each iter anyway. Use adaptive: if score improves, increase η slightly; else decrease. Use best-so-far parent. We can do line search: try a few η (0.01,0.03,0.1) and choose best.

But multiplicative with η=0.1 and top K may cause mass migrate to endpoints and objective worse if no backtracking. We can keep parent and only accept if score < best. For local search, we can perform candidate updates from parent and accept improvements. This becomes stochastic local search.

Algorithm:
```
parent = initial normalized
best, best_score
eta = 0.05
K = max(32, n//32)
while time:
  y=conv(parent)
  score=2n*y.max()
  choose top K
  H=...
  for candidate_eta in [eta*0.5, eta, eta*2]:
      cand = parent * exp(-eta_c*H/Hscale)
      cand += small? normalize
      y=conv(cand)
      sc=2n*y.max()
  if best candidate improves parent: parent=cand; update best; maybe keep eta
  else: eta*=0.7; if many fails random restart perturbation
```
This uses multiple conv per iter (3). For n=2000, FFT fast; maybe 1000 iters in min. Good.

Alternative: **solve continuous flat convolution via iteration in log** may converge to near optimum. The multiplicative gradient is essentially maximizing the minimum? def smooth_obj(theta):
    a = softmax(theta)
    t = conv_self(a)
    z = beta*t
    maxz = z.max()
    p = np.exp(z - maxz)
    p /= p.sum()
    val = (maxz + log(p.sum()))/beta + penalty? # this is logsumexp/beta
    return val
```

Let's design final code architecture:
```
import numpy as np
import time
import math
from scipy.optimize import minimize

best_score_global = np.inf
best_seq_global = None

def next_pow2(x): return 1 << (max(1,int(x)).bit_length())

def safe_softmax(theta):
    theta = np.asarray(theta, float).copy()
    if not np.isfinite(theta).all(): return None
    # center and clip
    theta -= theta.mean()
    theta = np.clip(theta, -50.0, 50.0)
    m = theta.max()
    z = np.exp(theta-m)
    s = z.sum()
    if s <=0 or not finite: return None
    return z/s

def conv_self_fft(a, N=None):
    n=a.size
    if N is None: N=next_pow2(2*n-1)
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a,N)
    out=np.fft.irfft(fa*fa,N)[:2*n-1]
    return out

def exact_score_arr(a):
    # sum not necessarily 1, but scale invariant
    a=np.asarray(a,float)
    if a.size==0 or not finite: return inf
    S=a.sum()
    if S < 0.01: return inf
    # Could use FFT for score to avoid direct in search. Exact same within error. Use direct for final?
    # Use FFT for internal; scale exactly: M from FFT may slight. For final, use evaluate_sequence.
    M = conv_self_fft(a/N? no). If a sum not1, M scales. score=2nM/S^2.
```
For internal, use normalized sum=1 so score=2*max(t). Use FFT. For final exact, call evaluate_sequence(list) maybe, but can call multiple times? okay. To avoid time, use np.convolve direct on final candidate? They will evaluate; but we can call to know. Since allowed many. Use `np.convolve` direct for small n? For n=2048 direct 4M, okay. Objective function for L-BFGS:
```
def smooth_fun(theta, n, beta, Nt, Nh, bounds=None):
    a = safe_softmax(theta)
    if a is None: return (np.inf, np.zeros(n))
    # t via FFT
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, Nt)
    t = np.fft.irfft(fa*fa, Nt)[:2*n-1]
    # due numerical small neg; for logsum, can subtract max
    maxt = float(t.max())
    if not finite: return inf
    z = np.exp(beta*(t - maxt))
    s = z.sum()
    if s <=0 or not finite: return...
    val = 2.0 * n * (maxt + math.log(s)/beta)
    # H
    # p = z/s
    p = z/s
    # H via FFT
    arev = a[::-1]
    N = Nh
    fp=np.fft.rfft(p,N)
    fr=np.fft.rfft(arev,N)
    H=np.fft.irfft(fp*fr,N)[n-1:2*n-1] # length n
    G=2.0*H
    avg=float(np.dot(a,G))
    grad = 2.0*n*a*(G - avg)
    # Remove any nonfinite
    if not np.isfinite(grad).all(): grad = np.nan_to_num(grad)
    # subtract small mean to enforce sum-zero
    grad -= grad.mean()
    return val, grad
```
Wait N for H: length conv(p, arev) = (2n-1)+n-1=3n-2. Slicing [n-1: n-1+n] = [n-1:2n-1], length n. Good.

But `np.fft.irfft` length N returns N; we need N≥3n-2. Use Nh = next_pow2(3*n-2). For n=512, 1534 ->2048. Nt next_pow2(1023)->1024.

If beta large, z may underflow for non-max; okay. p supported on max bins. Gradient approximates max subgradient. If t has flat max, softmax distributes.

L-BFGS `ftol` on val; val ~ score ~1.5-2.

For `smooth_fun`, top-level:
```
def _smooth_fun(theta, n, beta, Nt, Nh):
   th = np.asarray(theta, dtype=np.float64)
   if th.size != n: return (np.inf, np.zeros(n))
   th = th - np.mean(th)
   # clip for safety
   th = np.clip(th, -40.0, 40.0)
   m = th.max()
   z = np.exp(th-m)
   s = z.sum()
   if not np.isfinite(s) or s ==0: return inf
   a=z/s
   # t
   fa=np.fft.rfft(a,Nt)
   t=np.fft.irfft(fa*fa,Nt)[:2*n-1]
   # remove tiny imags? irfft real
   maxt=float(np.max(t))
   if not np.isfinite(maxt): ...
   # For numerical, if maxt <= 1e-12? return? but sum1 so max>0
   ex = np.exp(beta*(t - maxt))
   s2 = ex.sum()
   if s2 <=0 or not finite: ...
   val = 2.0*n*(maxt + math.log(s2)/beta)
   p = ex/s2
   arev = a[::-1]
   fp = np.fft.rfft(p, Nh)
   fr = np.fft.rfft(arev, Nh)
   H = np.fft.irfft(fp*fr, Nh)[n-1:2*n-1]
   G = 2.0*H
   avg = float(np.dot(a,G))
   grad = 2.0*n*a*(G - avg)
   if not np.all(np.isfinite(grad)):
       grad = np.nan_to_num(grad, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
   grad -= float(np.mean(grad))
   return val, grad
```
But `np.all(np.isfinite(grad))` with large n each call overhead; okay.
Use `math.log(s2)`; s2 can be huge? t-maxt<=0, beta* ≤0, z<=1, s2<=L ~n, log fine.
`val` ~2n maxt + small; maxt ~M ~score/(2n) ~0.001. val ~2. For n=1024, 2n=2048, maxt~0.00075 =>1.5.
Actually t max for sum1 is M; val = 2nM. Good.

For beta small, logsumexp smooth includes low bins; val slightly >max? logsumexp/beta = max + (log sum exp(-β(max-t)))/β, which approximates max + average(-?)/? For β small, approximates average t (since logsumexp(βt)/β -> mean t as β→0). So val tends to average objective=2n/(2n-1)~1. At β=5, not too sharp.

L-BFGS needs gradient of val with β factor included. p = exp(β(t-max))/sum; val = 2n(max + log s/beta). d val/d a = 2n * (log s/beta)' = 2n * ∑ p dt = 2n G. good. The `max` term derivative zero if β fixed but p max correction standard.

When `beta` very large and t has multiple close max, p okay.

Line search in L-BFGS may evaluate theta where a all concentrated; t maybe near a_i^2; score maybe large. Value finite.

Potential issue: `t` from FFT can have small negative values; for logsum, okay. But p for negative max? `max` positive. H from p nonnegative. If t negative tiny, p small. Good.

    if time > deadline: break

    x = res.x

    print label beta res.fun
return a
```

def _seed_uniform(n, rng=None):
  if rng is None: return zeros
  return rng.normal(0, 0.05, n)
def _seed_simple(n, rng):
  i=np.arange(n)
  choices = [
     np.zeros(n),
     np.log(np.asarray(i+1, float)),
     -np.log(np.asarray(i+1, float)),
     (n-i).astype(float)/n * 2.0,
     i.astype(float)/n * 2.0 -1,
     np.sin(2*np.pi*(n//3)*i/n),
     ...
  ]

But optimized seeds should be diverse. We can generate list for a stage:
```
def _make_seed_array(n, idx, rng, hs=None):
  if idx==0: return rng.normal(0,0.1,n)
  if idx==1: linear up: 2*(np.arange(n)-n/2)/n
  if idx==2: linear down
  if idx==3: parabola up: ((i/(n-1)-0.5)**2)*4 -2
  if idx==4: cosine mode 2: np.cos(2π*2*i/n)
  if idx==5: lowpass noise: _random_smooth(n,rng,width=n//20,amp=1)
  if idx==6: lowpass noise width n//5
  if idx==7: hs interpolated
```
Need ensure finite.

`_random_smooth(n,rng,width,amp)`:
```
x = rng.normal(0,1,n)
if width >1:
 ksize = int(width*6+1)
 if ksize > n: ksize=n
 sigma=width
 xs=np.arange(-ksize//2,ksize//2+1)
 k=np.exp(-xs**2/(2*sigma**2)); k/=k.sum()
 # cyclic convolution to keep length n
 X=np.fft.rfft(x); K=np.fft.rfft(k,n); x=np.fft.irfft(X*K,n)
return amp*x
```
For n large, ksize maybe 100, okay. Or use FFT filter.

`_seed_from_hs(hs, n)`: normalize and log. If not hs, return smooth noise.
```
if hs:
  arr = np.asarray(hs,float)
  arr = arr[np.isfinite(arr)]
  if arr.size>0:
    arr = np.clip(arr,0,1000)
    if arr.sum()<1e-12: return random
    # resample to n by linear interpolation on index centers
    pts = (np.arange(n)+0.5)*arr.size/n
    vals = np.interp(pts, np.arange(arr.size), arr)
    vals = np.clip(vals, 1e-6, None)
    theta = np.log(vals)
    theta -= theta.mean()
    return theta
```

def _refine_mult(a, deadline, label):

  Nt, Nh precompute

  scale=0.05

    idx = np.argsort(t)[-K:] if K<n else arange
    p_top = np.ones(len(idx)); p_top / len
    # create full p? For H direct faster:
    H=np.zeros(n)
    for k,w in zip(idx,p_top/K): H[lo:hi+1] += w * a_seg
    # add small background maybe uniform p over all bins? Hbg = (2/(2n-1))∑_{k} a_{k-i}, which is for each i sum of a over k-i valid = i? can compute as prefix? But skip, top K covers enough maybe endpoints not; to avoid extremes, add i-dependent? If top K only center when symmetric? H maybe central only. Add background: H += bg * a_total_near? For each i, ∑_{k valid over all k} a_{k-i} = ∑_{j=0}^{n-1} a[j] for k=i+j all 0..2n-2, so exactly S=1. Wait if p uniform over all 2n-1 bins, H_i = S/(2n-1) =1/(2n-1) constant. Thus G constant, grad zero. Add background with small weight ε: p_all uniform over all bins gives H=ε. This reduces all a equally? In multiplicative unnormalized, constant affects all equally and then normalized; harmless. For top K direct, some i not covered, H=0; they increase relative? Multiplicative: H=0 => no decrease, so mass moves to uncovered (could endpoints). Add background uniform epsilon so uncovered get small decrease. Good.
    H += epsilon (constant)
    a_new = a * exp(-eta * H)
    normalize
    if score improves accept else reduce eta
```

def _seed_array(n, idx, rng, hs):
    if idx == 0:
       return rng.normal(0,0.05,n)
    if idx == 1:
       if hs: return _seed_from_hs(hs,n)
       return rng.normal(0.5,n)
    if idx == 2:
       return _random_smooth_theta(n,rng,max(2,n//32),1.0)
    if idx == 3:
       return _random_smooth_theta(n,rng,max(2,n//8),1.5)
    if idx == 4:
       i=np.arange(n,dtype=float)
       return (i/(n-1)-0.5)*2.0
    if idx == 5:
       i=np.arange(n,float)
       return -((i/(n-1)-0.5)**2)*4.0  # high center? Actually high center theta positive middle; maybe good.
    if idx == 6:
       return np.cos(2*np.pi*2*i/n)
    if idx == 7:
       return np.log((i+1)**0.7)
    # fallback
    return rng.normal(0,1,n)
```
Normalize center at end? `_run_smooth` centers.

`_seed_from_hs`:
```
def _seed_from_hs(hs, n):
   arr=np.asarray(hs,dtype=float)
   arr=arr[np.isfinite(arr)]
   if arr.size==0: return _RNG.normal(0,1,n)
   arr=np.clip(arr,0,1000)
   if arr.sum() <=1e-12: return zeros
   # If arr length > 0
   pts=(np.arange(n)+0.5)*arr.size/n
   vals=np.interp(pts, np.arange(arr.size), arr)
   vals=np.clip(vals,1e-6,1000)
   th=np.log(vals)
   th-=th.mean()
   return th
```
If arr length n, interp unchanged.

`_random_smooth_theta`:
```
def _random_smooth_theta(n, rng, width, amp):
   x = rng.normal(0,1,n)
   if width > 1:
      sig = max(1.0, float(width))
      ksize = int(min(n, 8*sig*2+1)) # maybe 8σ
      if ksize %2 ==0: ksize+=1
      half=ksize//2
      xs=np.arange(-half, half+1)
      k=np.exp(-0.5*(xs/sig)**2)
      k/=k.sum()
      X=np.fft.rfft(x)
      K=np.fft.rfft(k,n)
      x=np.fft.irfft(X*K,n)
   th=amp*x
   th-=th.mean()
   return th
```

Let's test gradient scaling: `val = 2*n*(maxt + math.log(s2)/beta)`. If `s2` is sum exp(β(t-maxt)), log(s2) negative or zero. d/d t max term? The derivative of logsumexp normalized is p; d val/d a = 2n * (β p * dt)/(β) = 2n p^T dt? Actually val = 2n*(maxt + log(s2)/β). derivative of maxt term not zero? More formally logsumexp = maxt + log(s2). derivative wrt a = β Σ p dt. Multiplying 1/β gives Σ p dt. The derivative of maxt is included in p? Standard: d[logsumexp(βt)]/da = β Σ p dt. d[(1/β)logsumexp] = Σ p dt. So val = 2n * that; derivative = 2n Σ p_k ∂t_k/∂a = 2n G. Yes. Our formula for val using `maxt + log(s2)/beta` derivative okay. Good.

`G = 2*H`; ∂F_smooth/a = G? Since F_smooth = (1/β)logsumexp. Yes derivative = Σp ∂t = G (because ∂t=2H). Good.

Gradient θ from softmax: `grad_unit = a*(G - avgG)`. This is derivative of F_smooth wrt θ, not val? F_smooth = (1/β)logsumexp. But val=2n*F_smooth. So `_smooth_fun` val=2n*F_smooth, grad=2n*grad_unit. We return `grad = 2*n*a*(G-avg)`. Good.

Let's write code fully now in analysis to inspect.

```
import numpy as np
import time
import math
from scipy.optimize import minimize

_BEST_SCORE = float('inf')
_BEST_SEQ = None
_BEST_N = 0
_RNG = np.random.default_rng(12345)
_STOP_DEADLINE = 0.0

def _next_pow2(x):
    x = int(max(1, x))
    return 1 << (x - 1).bit_length()
```
Wait if x=1, (x-1).bit_length() =0 ->1. If x=2 ->1<<1=2. Good. But if x=0, max1.

```
def _softmax(theta):
    th = np.asarray(theta, dtype=np.float64)
    if th.size == 0: return None
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(th)): th = np.nan_to_num(th, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    th = th - float(np.mean(th))
    th = np.clip(th, -40.0, 40.0)
    m = float(np.max(th))
    z = np.exp(th - m)
    s = float(np.sum(z))
    if s <= 0 or not np.isfinite(s): return None
    a = z / s
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(a)):
        a = np.nan_to_num(a, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
        s = a.sum()
        if s <=0: return None
        a = a/s
    return a
```

`_conv_self_fft`:
```
def _conv_self_fft(a, N):
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, N)
    out = np.fft.irfft(fa * fa, N)
    return out
```
Call with N and slice outside. To avoid allocation full N? okay.

`_fast_score`:
```
def _fast_score(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,float)
    if a.size==0: return inf
    n=a.size
    N=_next_pow2(2*n-1)
    t=_conv_self_fft(a,N)[:2*n-1]
    return 2.0*n*float(np.max(t))
```
If sum not normalized, this doesn't divide by S^2. We should normalize. Either caller normalized or function:
```
S=a.sum(); if S<=1e-12 return inf; a=a/S; return 2n*max(conv)
```
Since scaling t by a^2; if we normalize a, score correct. But normalizing array each call cost. We'll use for unnormalized seeds maybe. In search, a sum1. Implement robust:
```
def _fast_score(a):
   a=np.nan_to_num(a,...); n=a.size; S=a.sum(); if S<1e-12 inf; a=a/S; ...
```
But this normalizes; for n=4096 and many calls, division cost small. Use this.

`_exact_score_list`:
```
def _exact_score_list(seq):
   try:
      score = float(evaluate_sequence(seq))
      return score
   except Exception:
      return float('inf')
```
`evaluate_sequence` global.

`_update_best`:
```
def _update_best(a, label="best", force_exact=False):
   global _BEST_SCORE, _BEST_SEQ, _BEST_N
   a=np.asarray(a,float)
   if a.size==0: return
   a=np.nan_to_num(a, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
   a=np.clip(a,0.0,1000.0)
   S=a.sum()
   n=a.size
   if S < 0.01:
      if n ==0: return
      a=np.ones(n,dtype=float)
      S=n
   else:
      a=a/S
   fs=_fast_score(a)
   if not np.isfinite(fs): return
   if not force_exact and fs > _BEST_SCORE + 1e-7:
      return
   seq=[float(x) for x in a]
   es=_exact_score_list(seq)
   if not np.isfinite(es): return
   if es < _BEST_SCORE - 1e-12:
       _BEST_SCORE=es; _BEST_SEQ=a.copy(); _BEST_N=n
       print(f"{label}: updated best n={n} score={es:.6f}")
```
This calls exact when fast score not worse than best by margin. If best inf, force exact. Good. If fs is slightly lower due FFT error but exact worse, no update. It still exact maybe many times if fs around best. acceptable.

`_upsample_seq`:
```
def _upsample_seq(a, n):
    m=a.size
    if m==n: return a.copy()
    if m==1: return np.full(n, a[0])
    x_old = (np.arange(m)+0.5)/m
    x_new = (np.arange(n)+0.5)/n
    vals = np.interp(x_new, x_old, a)
    vals = np.clip(vals, 0.0, None)
    S=vals.sum()
    if S <=0: vals=np.ones(n)
    else: vals/=S
    return vals
```

def propose_candidate(seed=0, budget_s=2400.0, **kwargs):
    global _BEST_SCORE, _BEST_SEQ, _BEST_N, _RNG, _PROPOSE_DEADLINE
    try: budget_s = float(budget_s)
    except: budget_s = 2400.0
    _PROPOSE_DEADLINE = time.monotonic() + max(10.0, float(budget_s) - 45.0)
    _RNG = np.random.default_rng(int(seed))
    try:
       hs = height_sequence_1
       if not isinstance(hs, (list, tuple, np.ndarray)) or len(hs)==0: hs=None
    except Exception: hs=None
    # initialize best with uniform 256
    _BEST_SCORE = float('inf'); _BEST_SEQ=None; _BEST_N=0
    init = np.ones(256)/256.0
    _update_best(init, "init")
    stages = [...]
    for n, nseeds, schedule, maxfun in stages:
       if time.monotonic() >= _PROPOSE_DEADLINE - 120: break
       for s in range(nseeds):
           if time.monotonic() >= _PROPOSE_DEADLINE - 60: break
           th = _seed_array(n, s, hs)
           label=f"n={n} seed={s}"
           x,a = _run_smooth(n, th, schedule, maxfun, label)
           # maybe after each seed if best very low continue? no
    # if time left and best exists, upscale
    if _BEST_SEQ is not None and time.monotonic() < _PROPOSE_DEADLINE - 120:
       n_cur = _BEST_N
       if n_cur == 0: ...
       if n_cur < 1024: n_up = max(1024, 2*n_cur)
       elif n_cur < 2048: n_up = 2048
       elif n_cur < 4096: n_up = 4096
       else: n_up = n_cur
       if n_up > n_cur and n_up <= 4096:
          a_up = _upsample_seq(_BEST_SEQ, n_up)
          theta_up = np.log(np.maximum(a_up, 1e-300))
          theta_up -= theta_up.mean()
          _run_smooth(n_up, theta_up, [40.0, 120.0], [200, 250], "upsample")
    # final high beta best
    if _BEST_SEQ is not None and time.monotonic() < _PROPOSE_DEADLINE - 60:
       th = np.log(np.maximum(_BEST_SEQ,1e-300)); th-=th.mean()
       _run_smooth(_BEST_N, th, [200.0], [200], "final")
    # fallback
    if _BEST_SEQ is None:
        seq=np.ones(256,dtype=float)
        return [float(v)/256.0 for v in seq] # sum1
    seq=_BEST_SEQ.astype(float)
    seq=np.clip(seq,0.0,1000.0)
    s=seq.sum()
    if s<0.01:
       seq=np.ones(max(1,seq.size),dtype=float); s=seq.size
    else:
       seq=seq/s
    return [float(v) for v in seq]
```

```
def _seed_array(n, idx, hs):
    rng = _RNG
    i = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64)
    if idx % 8 == 0: return rng.normal(0.0,0.05,n)
    if idx % 8 == 1:
       if hs is not None: return _seed_from_hs(hs,n,rng)
       return rng.normal(0.0,1.0,n)
    if idx % 8 == 2:
       return _random_smooth_theta(n, rng, max(2.0, n/32.0), 1.0)
    if idx % 8 == 3:
       return _random_smooth_theta(n, rng, max(2.0, n/8.0), 1.5)
    if idx % 8 == 4:
       if n > 1: return (i/(n-1.0) - 0.5)*2.0
       zeros
    if idx % 8 == 5:
       if n>1: x=i/(n-1)-0.5; return -4.0*x*x  # high center
    if idx % 8 == 6:
       return np.cos(2*np.pi*2*i/n)
    if idx % 8 == 7:
       return np.log((i+1.0)**0.7)
```

`_random_smooth_theta` with sigma based on float width. In `_seed_array` pass sigma not width. Use distribution width.
```
def _random_smooth_theta(n, rng, sigma, amp):
    x = rng.normal(0,1,n)
    if n > 8:
       sig = max(2.0, float(sigma))
       ksize = int(min(n, 2*(8*sig)+1))
       if ksize <=0: ksize=1
       if ksize % 2 ==0: ksize +=1
       if ksize > n: ksize = n if n%2==1 else n-1
       half = ksize//2
       xs = np.arange(-half, half+1, dtype=float)
       k = np.exp(-0.5*(xs/sig)**2); k/=k.sum()
       X=np.fft.rfft(x); K=np.fft.rfft(k,n); x=np.fft.irfft(X*K,n)
    th=amp*x; th-=th.mean(); return th
```
If ksize > n and even? ok.

But we can use nan_to_num with defaults. Okay.

```python
import numpy as np
import time
import math
from scipy.optimize import minimize

"""
Algorithm summary
-----------------
The score is scale invariant, so we optimize probability vectors on the simplex.
We parameterize a = softmax(theta), making nonnegativity automatic.  The exact
objective is the max of the linear convolution a*a.  We optimize a smooth
log-sum-exp approximation of that maximum with L-BFGS-B, using an annealed
sharpness beta.  Many random/structured seeds and several sequence lengths are
used.  The best candidate found so far is periodically and finally exact-checked
with evaluate_sequence().

Top-level helpers are used; define propose_candidate() for the harness.
"""

_BEST_SCORE = float("inf")
_BEST_SEQ = None
_BEST_N = 0
_RNG = np.random.default_rng(1234567)
_PROPOSE_DEADLINE = 0.0
_LAST_EXACT_CHECK = 0.0


def _next_pow2(x):
    x = int(max(1, x))
    return 1 << (x - 1).bit_length()


def _stop_callback(*args):
    # Generic callback for scipy.optimize.minimize.
    return time.monotonic() >= _PROPOSE_DEADLINE - 25.0


def _softmax(theta):
    th = np.asarray(theta, dtype=np.float64)
    if th.size == 0:
        return None
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(th)):
        th = np.nan_to_num(th, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    th = th - float(np.mean(th))
    th = np.clip(th, -40.0, 40.0)
    m = float(np.max(th))
    z = np.exp(th - m)
    s = float(np.sum(z))
    if s <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(s):
        return None
    a = z / s
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(a)):
        a = np.nan_to_num(a, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
        s = float(np.sum(a))
        if s <= 0.0:
            return None
        a = a / s
    return a


def _conv_self_fft(a, N):
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, N)
    return np.fft.irfft(fa * fa, N)


def _fast_score(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    if a.size == 0:
        return float("inf")
    a = np.nan_to_num(a, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, None)
    s = float(np.sum(a))
    if s < 1e-14:
        return float("inf")
    a = a / s
    n = a.size
    N = _next_pow2(2 * n - 1)
    t = _conv_self_fft(a, N)[:2 * n - 1]
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(t)):
        return float("inf")
    return 2.0 * float(n) * float(np.max(t))


def _exact_score_list(seq):
    global _LAST_EXACT_CHECK
    try:
        sc = float(evaluate_sequence(seq))
        _LAST_EXACT_CHECK = time.monotonic()
        return sc
    except Exception:
        return float("inf")


def _update_best(a, label, force_exact=False):
    global _BEST_SCORE, _BEST_SEQ, _BEST_N, _LAST_EXACT_CHECK

    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    if a.size == 0:
        return
    a = np.nan_to_num(a, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, None)
    s = float(np.sum(a))
    if s < 0.01:
        if a.size == 0:
            return
        a = np.ones(a.size, dtype=np.float64)
        s = float(a.size)
    a = a / s

    fs = _fast_score(a)
    if not np.isfinite(fs):
        return

    # Skip many exact calls if we are not clearly improving.
    if not force_exact:
        if fs > _BEST_SCORE + 1e-7:
            return
        if (
            _BEST_SCORE != float("inf")
            and fs > _BEST_SCORE - 1e-4
            and (time.monotonic() - _LAST_EXACT_CHECK) < 0.25
        ):
            return

    seq = [float(x) for x in a]
    es = _exact_score_list(seq)
    if not np.isfinite(es):
        return
    if es < _BEST_SCORE - 1e-12:
        _BEST_SCORE = es
        _BEST_SEQ = a.copy()
        _BEST_N = int(a.size)
        print(f"{label}: exact score {es:.6f}, n={a.size}")


def _smooth_fun(theta, n, beta, Nt, Nh):
    # Objective and gradient of
    #   2*n * (1/beta) * log(sum_k exp(beta*(a*a)_k))
    # for a = softmax(theta).
    th = np.asarray(theta, dtype=np.float64)
    if th.size != n:
        return float("inf"), np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)

    th = np.nan_to_num(th, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    th = th - float(np.mean(th))
    th = np.clip(th, -40.0, 40.0)

    m = float(np.max(th))
    z = np.exp(th - m)
    ss = float(np.sum(z))
    if ss <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(ss):
        return float("inf"), np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    a = z / ss

    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, Nt)
    t = np.fft.irfft(fa * fa, Nt)[: 2 * n - 1]
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(t)):
        return float("inf"), np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)

    mt = float(np.max(t))
    if not np.isfinite(mt):
        return float("inf"), np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)

    # Use a very large beta to approximate the true max while keeping
    # log-sum-exp stable.
    b = float(beta)
    e = np.exp(b * (t - mt))
    se = float(np.sum(e))
    if se <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(se):
        return float("inf"), np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)

    val = 2.0 * float(n) * (mt + math.log(se) / b)

    p = e / se
    H = np.fft.irfft(np.fft.rfft(p, Nh) * np.fft.rfft(a[::-1], Nh), Nh)[n - 1 : 2 * n - 1]
    G = 2.0 * H  # d/d a of the smooth objective part

    avgG = float(np.dot(a, G))
    grad_theta_unit = a * (G - avgG)

    grad = 2.0 * float(n) * grad_theta_unit
    grad = np.nan_to_num(grad, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    grad -= float(np.mean(grad))
    return val, grad


def _run_smooth(n, theta0, schedule, maxfun_list, label):
    Nt = _next_pow2(2 * n - 1)
    Nh = _next_pow2(3 * n - 2)
    bounds = [(-25.0, 25.0)] * n

    x = np.asarray(theta0, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    x = np.nan_to_num(x, nan=0.0, posinf=25.0, neginf=-25.0)
    x -= float(np.mean(x))
    x = np.clip(x, -25.0, 25.0)

    a = _softmax(x)
    if a is not None:
        _update_best(a, f"{label}:start", force_exact=False)

    for beta, mf in zip(schedule, maxfun_list):
        if time.monotonic() >= _PROPOSE_DEADLINE - 45.0:
            break
        mf = int(max(50, mf))
        options = {
            "maxiter": mf,
            "ftol": 1e-9,
            "gtol": 1e-7,
            "maxfun": mf,
            "maxls": 20,
        }
        try:
            res = minimize(
                _smooth_fun,
                x,
                args=(n, float(beta), Nt, Nh),
                method="L-BFGS-B",
                bounds=bounds,
                jac=True,
                callback=_stop_callback,
                options=options,
            )
        except Exception as exc:
            print(f"{label}: scipy minimize failed at beta={beta}: {exc}")
            break

        if hasattr(res, "x") and res.x is not None:
            x = np.asarray(res.x, dtype=np.float64)

        x = np.nan_to_num(x, nan=0.0, posinf=25.0, neginf=-25.0)
        x -= float(np.mean(x))
        x = np.clip(x, -25.0, 25.0)
        a = _softmax(x)
        if a is not None:
            _update_best(a, f"{label}:beta={beta}", force_exact=False)

    return x, a


def _random_smooth_theta(n, rng, sigma, amp):
    x = rng.normal(0.0, 1.0, size=n)
    if n > 16:
        sig = max(2.0, float(sigma))
        ksize = int(min(n, 16.0 * sig + 1.0))
        if ksize <= 0:
            ksize = 1
        if ksize % 2 == 0:
            ksize += 1
        if ksize > n:
            ksize = n if (n % 2 == 1) else n - 1
        if ksize <= 0:
            ksize = 1
        half = ksize // 2
        xs = np.arange(-half, half + 1, dtype=np.float64)
        k = np.exp(-0.5 * (xs / sig) ** 2)
        k /= float(np.sum(k))
        X = np.fft.rfft(x)
        K = np.fft.rfft(k, n)
        x = np.fft.irfft(X * K, n)
    th = amp * x
    th -= float(np.mean(th))
    return th


def _seed_from_hs(hs, n, rng):
    try:
        arr = np.asarray(hs, dtype=np.float64)
    except Exception:
        return _random_smooth_theta(n, rng, max(2.0, n / 16.0), 1.0)

    arr = np.nan_to_num(arr, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    arr = np.clip(arr, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(np.sum(arr))
    if arr.size == 0 or s <= 1e-12:
        return np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)

    if arr.size == n:
        vals = arr
    else:
        x_old = (np.arange(arr.size) + 0.5) / float(arr.size)
        x_new = (np.arange(n) + 0.5) / float(n)
        vals = np.interp(x_new, x_old, arr)

    vals = np.nan_to_num(vals, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    vals = np.clip(vals, 1e-12, None)
    th = np.log(vals)
    th -= float(np.mean(th))
    return th


def _seed_array(n, idx, hs):
    rng = _RNG
    i = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64)
    k = idx % 8

    if k == 0:
        # Nearly uniform with small noise.
        return rng.normal(0.0, 0.05, size=n)

    if k == 1:
        if hs is not None:
            return _seed_from_hs(hs, n, rng)
        return _random_smooth_theta(n, rng, max(2.0, n / 16.0), 1.0)

    if k == 2:
        # Broad smooth random pattern.
        return _random_smooth_theta(n, rng, max(2.0, n / 32.0), 1.2)

    if k == 3:
        # Medium smooth random pattern.
        return _random_smooth_theta(n, rng, max(2.0, n / 8.0), 1.8)

    if k == 4:
        if n <= 1:
            return np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
        # Linear ramp up.
        return (i / (n - 1.0) - 0.5) * 2.0

    if k == 5:
        if n <= 1:
            return np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
        # Centered concave shape.
        x = i / (n - 1.0) - 0.5
        return -4.0 * x * x

    if k == 6:
        # Low cosine mode.
        return np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * 2.0 * i / float(n))

    # k == 7: weighted power ramp.
    return np.log((i + 1.0) ** 0.7)


def _upsample_seq(a, n):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    if a.size == 0:
        return np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(max(1, n))
    m = a.size
    if m == n:
        return a.copy()
    if m == 1:
        v = float(a[0])
        out = np.full(n, v, dtype=np.float64)
    else:
        x_old = (np.arange(m) + 0.5) / float(m)
        x_new = (np.arange(n) + 0.5) / float(n)
        out = np.interp(x_new, x_old, np.nan_to_num(a, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0))

    out = np.nan_to_num(out, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    out = np.clip(out, 0.0, None)
    s = float(np.sum(out))
    if s <= 1e-14:
        out = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
        s = float(n)
    return out / s


def propose_candidate(seed=0, budget_s=2400.0, **kwargs):
    global _BEST_SCORE, _BEST_SEQ, _BEST_N, _RNG, _PROPOSE_DEADLINE

    try:
        budget_s = float(budget_s)
    except Exception:
        budget_s = 2400.0
    if not np.isfinite(budget_s):
        budget_s = 2400.0
    budget_s = max(60.0, budget_s)

    _PROPOSE_DEADLINE = time.monotonic() + max(20.0, budget_s - 45.0)
    _RNG = np.random.default_rng(int(seed) if np.isfinite(seed) else 0)

    _BEST_SCORE = float("inf")
    _BEST_SEQ = None
    _BEST_N = 0

    try:
        hs = height_sequence_1
        if not isinstance(hs, (list, tuple, np.ndarray)) or len(hs) == 0:
            hs = None
    except Exception:
        hs = None

    # Basic valid fallback.
    _update_best(np.full(256, 1.0, dtype=np.float64) / 256.0, "fallback", force_exact=True)

    # Stages: (length, number of seeds, beta schedule, maxfun schedule)
    # These are intentionally conservative in function evaluations and scaled
    # to finish comfortably inside the 2400 second wall-clock budget.
    stages = [
        (512, 5, [20.0, 200.0, 2000.0], [300, 350, 400]),
        (1024, 3, [20.0, 200.0, 2000.0], [250, 300, 350]),
        (2048, 2, [50.0, 500.0, 5000.0], [180, 220, 260]),
        (4096, 1, [100.0, 1000.0, 10000.0], [140, 180, 220]),
    ]

    for n, nseeds, schedule, maxfun_schedule in stages:
        if time.monotonic() >= _PROPOSE_DEADLINE - 150.0:
            break
        for s in range(nseeds):
            if time.monotonic() >= _PROPOSE_DEADLINE - 70.0:
                break
            th = _seed_array(n, s, hs)
            label = f"stage n={n} seed={s}"
            _run_smooth(n, th, schedule, maxfun_schedule, label)

    # If we have time, try to enlarge and polish the currently best sequence.
    if _BEST_SEQ is not None and _BEST_N > 0 and time.monotonic() < _PROPOSE_DEADLINE - 180.0:
        n_cur = int(_BEST_N)
        if n_cur < 1024:
            n_up = max(1024, 2 * n_cur)
        elif n_cur < 2048:
            n_up = 2048
        elif n_cur < 4096:
            n_up = 4096
        else:
            n_up = n_cur

        if n_up > n_cur and n_up <= 4096:
            a_up = _upsample_seq(_BEST_SEQ, n_up)
            theta_up = np.log(np.maximum(a_up, 1e-300))
            theta_up -= float(np.mean(theta_up))
            _run_smooth(
                n_up,
                theta_up,
                [100.0, 1000.0, 10000.0],
                [200, 260, 320],
                f"upsample {n_cur}->{n_up}",
            )

    # Final high-sharpness polish of the best sequence we have.
    if _BEST_SEQ is not None and _BEST_N > 0 and time.monotonic() < _PROPOSE_DEADLINE - 80.0:
        th_final = np.log(np.maximum(_BEST_SEQ, 1e-300))
        th_final -= float(np.mean(th_final))
        _run_smooth(
            int(_BEST_N),
            th_final,
            [10000.0],
            [400],
            "final polish",
        )

    if _BEST_SEQ is None:
        out = np.ones(256, dtype=np.float64) / 256.0
        return [float(x) for x in out]

    out = np.asarray(_BEST_SEQ, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    out = np.nan_to_num(out, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    out = np.clip(out, 0.0, 1000.0)

    # Remove exact tiny endpoints, if any, and crop zero padding.
    out[out < 1e-12] = 0.0
    nz = np.nonzero(out > 0.0)[0]
    if nz.size > 0:
        out = out[nz[0] : nz[-1] + 1]

    s = float(np.sum(out))
    if out.size == 0 or s < 0.01:
        out = np.ones(256, dtype=np.float64) / 256.0
    else:
        out = out / s

    # Force a final authoritative exact update after cleanup.
    seq = [float(x) for x in out]
    sc = _exact_score_list(seq)
    if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < _BEST_SCORE:
        _BEST_SCORE = sc
        _BEST_SEQ = np.asarray(seq, dtype=np.float64)
        _BEST_N = out.size

    print(f"final returned length={out.size} approximate score={_BEST_SCORE:.6f}")
    return seq
```
